2428	=================== PT108-S2-Q21 | diff 3 | key C | score 16.4 | features: Famous Flaws, Rebuttals, Timeframes
2429	STIMULUS: The experts who now assure us that genetically engineered plants are harmless are the same experts who claimed that introducing non-native plants into the wild was a good idea. It is too late to be skeptical now that some non-native plants have become a serious problem. But we should conclude, while we still have time, that genetically engineered plants will also be harmful.
2430	STEM: The flawed reasoning in which one of the following most closely parallels the flawed reasoning in the argument above?
2431	(A) [2% | Bad Evidence Match] The same people who complain that taxes are too high complain that the government does not provide enough services. We should conclude that high taxes and big government go together.
2432	(B) [18% | Weak Evidence/Conclusion Match] The film critics who called Meisner’s last film brilliant are the same ones who are calling her new film confused and boring. But because Meisner’s last film was excellent I conclude that this one will be also.
2433	(C) [75% | Correct] The economists who tell us that the current economic expansion will soon be over are the same economists who failed to predict the end of the last recession. Wise investors will conclude that the expansion will continue for some time.
2434	(D) [3% | Bad Evidence Match] Children who beg and plead with their parents to buy them trendy toys are the same children who begged for trendy toys last year. I conclude that parents ought to ignore such pleadings and simply buy traditional toys.
2435	(E) [4% | Bad Conclusion Match] The population experts who are predicting world food shortages in the next decade are the same ones who have erroneously predicted such shortages in the past. Therefore, if there are food shortages in the next decade, it will not be because population experts predicted them.
2436	--- BREAKDOWN
2437	## Conclusion (we should conclude)
2438	Genetically engineered plants will also be harmful.
2439	## Evidence
2440	The experts who assure us that genetically engineered plants are harmless are the same who said that introducing non-native plants into the wild was a good idea (it wasn't / some of them have become a serious problem).
2441	## Evaluate
2442	This argument is essentially saying, "Since Max was wrong last year when he predicted who would win the Superbowl, and since this year he's saying the Cowboys will win it, we should conclude that the Cowboys will not win it".
2443	It's a flavor of the famous Ad Hominem flaw, where we dismiss a point of view because the source of that view has some ulterior motive / vested interest, or because the source of that view has some conflicting past behavior. This argument is an example of the latter. "Since this source was wrong before, they must be wrong again this time."
2444	## Goal
2445	We're looking for an argument that is saying Prem 1: The people saying X are the same people who previously said Y. Prem 2: Y turned out to be wrong. Conc: X will turn out to be wrong.
2446	--- CHOICE NOTES
2447	(A) We have "the same people who say X are the ones who say Y", which is a weak match for, "The same people who say X are the ones who previously said Y".
2448	
2449	But more importantly, there's no premise saying, "Y ended up being wrong."
2450	(B) We have "the same people who said X are the ones who now say Y", which is a good match for our first premise. Do we have a premise saying, "They were wrong about X" and a conclusion saying, "So they'll be wrong about Y?"
2451	
2452	No. We have a premise saying, "They were right about X (they said the last film was brilliant and it was excellent), so I think they'll be wrong about Y (they said the new film will suck but I think it will be good)."
2453	(C) We can match everything up here with the original.
2454	
2455	Prem 1: The people saying X are the same people who previously said Y.Those now saying "current expansion will soon be ending" are the same ones who said "recession isn't ending yet".Prem 2: Y turned out to be wrong.The recession did end. They failed to predict its end.Conc: X will turn out to be wrong.The current expansion is not ending. It will continue for some time.
2456	
2457	Notice how the two premises are sort of enfolded into the same fact. Correct answers like to play that trick to disguise their similarity. Whereas the original said, "They assured us non-native would be harmless. Now we know that some have become a serious problem.", this answer accomplishes the same feel of They Were Wrong in the Past a different way, saying, "they failed to predict the end of the last recession".
2458	
2459	It's akin to the original argument conveying what it says in the first two sentences by saying, "The experts who now assure us that genetically engineered plants are harmless are the same experts who failed to anticipate that non-native plants would become a serious problem."
2460	(D) We hear that the kids begging this year are the same as the kids who begged last year, but we aren't ever told that in the previous instance the kids were wrong to beg.
2461	(E) The evidence works great. The conclusion should be saying that their current prediction is wrong (since their old prediction was wrong).
2462	
2463	Their current prediction is that there will be food shortages in the next decade, so the conclusion should sound like:
2464	
2465	Thus, we won't be seeing any food shortages in the next decade.
2466	
2467	Instead, it's a conditional statement and it involves the funky concept of the food shortage being caused by the prediction itself.

What the best ones do\n\n- **Name the flaw first, then pick the tool.** For conditional or quantified stimuli, say the recipe alone can carry you even if the flaw is fuzzy (PT153-S3-Q11, PT122-S4-Q24). For story flaws, write the objection as the prediction: \"couldn't both be wrong?\" (PT117-S2-Q22), \"does Katelin even know that?\" (PT153-S2-Q25).\n- **Prove the flaw with small made-up numbers or a familiar example.** Dorm sizes (PT148-S1-Q24), a 100-person breakdown (PT142-S4-Q25), major sizes (PT146-S3-Q25), Clark Kent (PT153-S2-Q25), student-loan-to-mortgage (PT143-S3-Q14).\n- **Sort wrong answers into three kinds: valid, different flaw, or right flaw with the wrong structure.** PT107-S4-Q21 (A, D, E valid; B wrong conclusion); PT124-S2-Q8 (each wrong answer gets its own named flaw); PT155-S1-Q8 (A and D are the legal contrapositive plus a different slip).\n- **Rank flaw match over structure match, and say so.** PT155-S1-Q8 B (\"replicating the flaw is the most important part\"); PT142-S1-Q25 B (negation vs. reversal: same family, weaker match).\n- **Test the popular trap against the objection, not just the recipe.** PT116-S3-Q22 E (timeline blocks reverse causation); PT117-S2-Q22 A (\"at least one wrong\" already allows both); PT149-S4-Q25 E (\"only viable candidates\" closes the gap).\n- **Group wrong answers by the error they share.** PT142-S4-Q25 (B and C: \"person is not X\"; D and E: \"person is X\") teaches two trap shapes in two notes.\n- **Point out the vocabulary traps the question turns on.** Biconditional wording (PT148-S3-Q22 D); \"the students\" vs. \"the student body\" (PT111-S3-Q6 A); \"might believe\" and \"could be\" are too safe to be flawed (PT153-S2-Q25 D, PT122-S4-Q24 B).\n- **When a stimulus has two moves, say which one is flawed and which the answers will copy.** PT107-S3-Q23 (the first move is flawed twice, the second is fine); PT124-S2-Q8 (the gambler's step, not the recommendation).\n- **Show where the key hides its match.** Premises folded into one fact (PT108-S2-Q21 C); scrambled \"since X, Y\" order (PT122-S4-Q24 A, PT107-S4-Q21 C).\n\n### 3.
